Overview Trey, are you ready to launch your board?! It looks great, it's personalized, and it offers relevant content. Now is the time to create meaningful engagement! The most common way to launch a Folloze ABX campaign is to configure a link to the board and share it through various digital channels. Inbound, like paid or organic social and targeted ad campaigns, or outbound like marketing and sales emails. In this course we focus on configuring a sharable link and optimizing for a personalized experience that is fully trackable. Before You Get Started Prepare your tracking parameters: Whether you're using UTMs or other tracking parameters, make sure you have those parameter values available to you as you configure the link. By default, Folloze uses the common UTM scheme for tracking traffic sources, but you, as the user, are responsible for appending the proper UTM values to the links. Distinguish Link Types: Clearly understand the distinctions between different link types (Public Link, Campaign Link, Individual Trackable Link, Group Trackable Link) to tailor your sharing strategy. Match each link type for its specific use case, enhancing the effectiveness of your outreach efforts.
```

### Share a Board with a Link

#### Page 002 - Types of Board Links

- Screenshot: `002_share_a_board_with_a_link_types_of_board_links_6c9efb5c.png`

Table of Contents
List of activities in this course
INTRODUCTION
SHARE A BOARD WITH A LINK
SEND A BOARD WITH FOLLOZE +
OUTREACH API
CHECK YOUR UNDERSTANDING
Types of Board Links
Here are the various types of links available, each tailored for specific sharing strategies and audience engagement. All
links behave the same for sharing a board or sharing a specific content item.
Public Link - automatically generated links that do not carry identifying information about the board visitor. A visitor clicking a public
link will land on the board as an unknown visitor. Appending parameters to public links, like UTMs or other tracking parameters, is
recommended as a way to analyze traffic sources.
Campaign Link - a link that creates a connection between Folloze and email automation tools like Marketing Automation Platform,
Sales Development Platforms, or CRMs. The parameters appended to this link pull identifying information from your other platforms.
This information can be used for personalization purposes as well as for tracking.
Individual Trackable Link - used for an individual you may be emailing ad hoc or contacting on Linkedin; the recipient's email
address is required to create the unique token in this link.
Group Trackable Link - Create a unique link for a specific group. A common use case is tracking traffic from a specific group of
individuals from an organization when email addresses are not known.
Jump into the video below to learn more about sharing a Board with a link.

Append UTMs to Links
Appending UTMs to links enables tracking and filtering of source, medium, and campaign data, enhancing insights into
user interactions. By utilizing UTMs, marketers can accurately measure the success of different marketing channels
and optimize strategies based on data-driven insights. This allows for more effective allocation of resources to achieve
marketing goals.

Table of Contents
List of activities in this course
INTRODUCTION
FOLLOZE LICENSES EXPLAINED
>
Overview & Things to Keep in Mind
Folloze's User Management provides a robust self-service solution within the Company Admin, allowing Admins to view
and manage users, licenses, and roles.
Additionally, Company Admins can seamlessly oversee user assignments, teams, and roles, ensuring a cohesive
experience across the platform all while remaining compliant with your Folloze contract licensing terms.
Note: The number of licenses / users you can add will be dependent on your contract with Folloze. If you require additional licenses, please reach out to your
Customer Success Manager for assistance.
MANAGE USER ROLES, TEAMS, &
PERMISSIONS
CHECK YOUR UNDERSTANDING
Things to Keep in Mind
• Team Assignments for Collaboration:
• Assign each user to a specific team for streamlined collaboration.
• Strategic Team Creation:
• Work with your CSM to create teams aligned with business functions (e.g., AM East, AE Central) for optimized user
management.
• Leverage Teams to segment reporting, attributing contributions effectively and facilitating targeted analysis
• Efficient User Management:
• Regularly review and clean user assignments, suspend licenses when no longer needed, and ensure precise
assignment of collaborator and creator licenses
POWERED BY NORTHPASC
-
• 100%
CONTINUE >

Creator License Continued
As previously covered, the Creator license is typically granted to marketers. Let's take a closer look at each of
the roles within the Creator license:
INTRODUCTION
>
FOLLOZE LICENSES EXPLAINED
>
MANAGE USER ROLES, TEAMS, &
PERMISSIONS
>
• Company Admin: controls all admin settings and has access to all Boards and content.
• Company Editor: has access to all Boards and content.
• Creator Author: has access to Boards and content that they have permissions on.
CHECK YOUR UNDERSTANDING
>
• Creator Player: has access to Boards and content that they have permissions on. Can create a Board, but
needs approval to publish it.
• Creator Player (Limited): cannot create campaigns.

Your turn! Take the first step in managing user roles, teams, and permissions by following the steps below:
INTRODUCTION
FOLLOZE LICENSES EXPLAINED
>
MANAGE USER ROLES, TEAMS, &
PERMISSIONS
CHECK YOUR UNDERSTANDING
1. Navigate to the User Management tab in the Company Admin.
2. Invite a new user to your Folloze Instance.
- After filling in the required details, including email, click Invite user.
- Locate the newly invited user in the User Management tab.
3. Click on the user's row or use the menu icon to access User Details.
4. Assign a role to the user and save the changes.
5. Now find your own user row and add your photo.
- Optionally, include additional details like social media accounts, bio, and an email signature.

Introduction
Personalization for Growth Account-based Marketing (ABM) allows you to deliver targeted messaging at scale that will
resonate with your audience. In this course, you'll define segment tiers for a Growth ABM model and use personalization to
engage prospects at each stage of their buyer journey. By the end of this course, you'll be able to more effectively engage
prospects and guide them towards Marketing Qualified Account (MQA) and purchase.
Let's Dive into:
Personalization for Growth ABM
To get the most from this course, ensure you're already confident building Boards. We highly recommend that you complete
at least the Personalize Your Board course before continuing.
Before we jump straight into personalization strategy, it's also important to understand why the traditional pyramid-shaped
ABM models do not clearly reflect how ABM is executed today. Modern Account-Based Go-To-Market (ABGTM) strategy
has new deployment models: Enterprise ABM, Deal Acceleration, and Growth ABM. This course is all about how to
implement personalization for a Growth ABM strategy in ways that provide value to prospects.
Building personalization rules with an ABM strategy in place is more efficient or effective than creating personalization rules
without context. At Folloze, we understand that to get the most from personalization, you must understand your customers,
where they are in their journey, and personalize their experience in ways that provide them with true value.

Growth ABM Overview
Industry research from trusted sources highlights the evolution of ABM strategies in B2B organizations. This research
emphasizes the need for a scalable approach to ABM. We align with these findings and anticipate that this scalable
framework will become the new industry standard.

A Growth ABM model is especially effective for companies or business units that:
• Target hundreds to thousands of accounts within a specific segment
• Generate revenue in the hundreds of millions (or less)
• Experience sales cycles of approximately three months (or fewer)
• Spend around (or under) one thousand dollars per account on average
Any team that targets hundreds to thousands of accounts and wants to scale their ABM program should implement a
Growth ABM strategy.
ABM is most effective as a strategy, not a tactic.

Why Define Tiers
Within a Growth ABM framework, you should organize your resources and investment decisions strategically. To start,
define three main account tiers and transform your one-to-many approach. Growth ABM engages your entire target list
by scoring, prioritizing, and segmenting accounts into tiers. Each tier determines the personalization depth and tactical
approaches applied.
This system enables you to scale personalized experiences to hundreds or thousands of accounts while maintaining focus.
Tiering accounts helps optimize resources by directing your most intensive efforts where they'll generate the highest return.
Higher-tier accounts receive greater investment and personalization, while lower tiers get a more standardized (but still
targeted) approach. This allows your team to extend account-based strategies effectively across their entire target market.

Growth ABM:
Operates as a 1:Many model, specifically designed for engagement of the entirety of a target account list. This
methodology involves scoring and prioritizing accounts, and then segmenting accounts into tiers, often three in
number. The assigned tier dictates the extent of personalization and tactical eligibility an account receives, thereby
optimizing resource allocation and ensuring the most tailored efforts are made where they matter most.

Define your Tier 1 Accounts
Tier 1 accounts represent your highest priority accounts, which are 20x more likely to convert to an opportunity within three
months. According to 6sense, a good model should show a higher conversion rate for 6QA accounts compared to Baseline
and other buying stages.
You'll have fewer accounts in this tier compared to Tiers 2 and 3. No more than 5% of your accounts should be in the Tier 1
segment. You will want to dedicate more tactical resources, personalization, and investment to this tier.
Listen to Melinda Monaco, Senior Director of Revenue Marketing & Operations at Folloze, break down examples of how to
define your Tier 1 segment in the video below.

Define your Tier 2 Accounts
Tier 2 accounts represent your second level of priority accounts. They are still 5x more likely to convert to an opportunity
because they have a strong Profile Fit, but they require increased brand awareness and engagement to move to the next
journey stage.
You'll have more accounts in this tier than in Tier 1, but fewer than those in Tier 3. No more than 10% of your accounts
should be in the Tier 2 segment. You will want to reduce tactical resources and investment in this tier.
Listen to Melinda Monaco, Senior Director of Revenue Marketing & Operations at Folloze, break down an example of how to
define a Tier 2 segment in the video below.

Implement your Tiers
Now that you've defined your account tiers, you can use them to help determine how to allocate your resources.
Use your tiers for guidance to efficiently direct effort and budget.
Growth ABM Tiers
Tier 1
• More ad spend
• Custom content if possible
Tier 2
• Targeted content curation

Create a Scalable TOFU Experience
Consider your prospects and their needs. What are they looking for at each stage of the buyer journey? To increase
awareness and accelerate demand, you'll need to align each buyer journey stage with relevant content, copy, etc. This is the
first step to creating an effective, scalable TOFU experience.
At a high level, first you need to reach, then engage, and then convert prospects.
>
In the slide "Content by Journey Stage" (shown below), we outline how to balance cost-effective GTM motions with highly
personalized experiences so you can create a scalable TOFU experience.
>
>
PROGRESS
‹ BACK
CONTINUE >
leadership articles, educational infographics, industry reports, white papers, and brand awareness videos during this buyer
journey stage.
You can compare this to how you should cater to prospects at the very bottom of the top-of-funnel (aka in the Purchase
[6S] or MQA [DB] stage). Accounts at this stage are usually digging into the details and directly comparing solutions. They
want to make an educated decision with their buying team to select the solution that's best for them. Generally, their goal is
to schedule a demo or meeting, so help facilitate that with clear CTAs. It's best practice to use interactive tours, pricing
pages, FAQs, customer testimonials, and ROI Calculators for prospects at this stage of their buyer journey. It's also
important to address the various personas on the buying team.
```

Structured Academy text:

```text
Create a Scalable TOFU Experience Consider your prospects and their needs. What are they looking for at each stage of the buyer journey? To increase awareness and accelerate demand, you’ll need to align each buyer journey stage with relevant content, copy, etc. This is the first step to creating an effective, scalable TOFU experience. At a high level, first you need to reach, then engage, and then convert prospects. In the slide "Content by Journey Stage" (shown below), we outline how to balance cost-effective GTM motions with highly personalized experiences so you can create a scalable TOFU experience. At the very top-of-funnel (aka in the Target [6S] or All Other [DB] stage), your goal should be to build brand familiarity and credibility. You can achieve this by showing that you understand each prospect’s challenges or needs and how your product addresses them. Since the goal is to increase brand familiarity and credibility, it’s best practice to highlight thought leadership articles, educational infographics, industry reports, white papers, and brand awareness videos during this buyer journey stage. You can compare this to how you should cater to prospects at the very bottom of the top-of-funnel (aka in the Purchase [6S] or MQA [DB] stage). Accounts at this stage are usually digging into the details and directly comparing solutions. They want to make an educated decision with their buying team to select the solution that’s best for them. Generally, their goal is to schedule a demo or meeting, so help facilitate that with clear CTAs. It’s best practice to use interactive tours, pricing pages, FAQs, customer testimonials, and ROI Calculators for prospects at this stage of their buyer journey. It's also important to address the various personas on the buying team.
```

Buyer Journey Stages in Folloze
Now that you've considered how to group your accounts into three tiers, you can start building suitable buyer experiences
for each tier within Folloze.
Folloze has integrations with top ABM data intelligence platforms: 6sense [6S] and Demandbase [DB]. Your data provider
can help you segment your accounts intelligently, identify leads, give you access to unique fields to trigger personalization
→
For Growth ABM, we focus on personalization for each buyer journey stage so we can support buyers by providing
relevant content to make their decision-making process easier.
Below, we've outlined each buyer journey stage that can be used to trigger personalization in Folloze.
• If you're a Demandbase user, you may know these as Journey Stages.
• If you're a 6sense user, you may know these as In-Market Stages or Buying Stages.
See how Folloze personalizes Boards via buying stage fields in the video below.

Account Protile Hit [65] assesses now closely a target customer matcnes your ideal Customer Protle (ICP), considering
factors like firmographics, technographics, and buying group personas. For example, you can use Account Profile Fit [6S] to
limit paid media spend for the Target [6S] journey stage and prioritize accounts aligned with your ICP. This focuses media
spend on accounts we know have a higher propensity of engaging and converting through the top-of-funnel. It's a smarter
and more impactful way to allocate paid media dollars.
Account Segments [6S] refine your ICP by additional criteria or signals, creating valuable sub-segments for targeted
personalized buyer content. For example, Folloze uses the technographic attribute to target a sub-segment that uses a
specific technology partner. Then we deliver tailored "Better Together" ads and direct relevant prospects to a personalized
Folloze Board featuring joint customer stories.

Create your Default Board
Your default Board design will be displayed to all unknown prospects that land on your Board. It should be relevant to your
whole segment to address scenarios where no personalization rules are triggered.
Cater to the Consideration [6S] or Aware [DB] buyer journey stage as you design your default Board. Generally, these stages
cover a large percentage of prospects. You can have a strong impact on unknown leads by using your default Board to
create an engaging experience for prospects earlier in their buyer journey.
Prospects later in their buyer journey are more likely to be or become known and therefore trigger personalization for
specific buyer stages like Decision [6S] or Engaged [DB], etc. (This is another reason why it's logical to design default
Boards for prospects earlier in their journey.)
Note: Your default Board design will be copied whenever you create a new personalization rule. It's best to have your default Board finalized before adding
personalization rules. If you make design changes while working on your default Board, you'll usually need to make those changes for both the default Board and
any relevant personalization rules.
PROGRESS
• 100% |
< BACK
CONTINUE >
While designing your default Board's copy and layout, you may intuitively think about what and where to personalize content
or design for specific accounts. Jot down these thoughts as you have them. You may be able to implement them into your
personalization strategy later on.
What would you like to be different from your default Board? Who would you like it to be different for and when?
To create an effective personalization strategy, you'll need to take a step back and ask yourself; will this specific change
help engage certain prospects? Take time to refine your thoughts and implement them as part of your personalization
```

Cater to the Consideration [6S] or Aware [DB] buyer journey stage as you design your default Board. Generally, these stages cover a large percentage of prospects. You can have a strong impact on unknown leads by using your default Board to create an engaging experience for prospects earlier in their buyer journey. Prospects later in their buyer journey are more likely to be or become known and therefore trigger personalization for specific buyer stages like Decision [6S] or Engaged [DB], etc. (This is another reason why it’s logical to design default Boards for prospects earlier in their journey.) Note: Your default Board design will be copied whenever you create a new personalization rule. It’s best to have your default Board finalized before adding personalization rules. While designing your default Board’s copy and layout, you may intuitively think about what and where to personalize content or design for specific accounts. Jot down these thoughts as you have them. Create Rule Sets that Scale
If you had only one account to focus on, it would be easy to craft ultra-personalized content! However, being too specific
with your personalization rules is not scalable. Most teams need to target many prospects — all at various stages of their
buyer journey...
20% of your effort results
in 80% of your results.
20%
80%
So, when creating personalization rule sets, keep the 80/20 rule in mind. Build your strategy around your use of
personalization that will be impactful to the most people with the least manual effort. This will keep your approach
scalable.
Note: You can also carefully select where and for whom to invest in hyper-specific rules (those are generally reserved for Tier 1 or high-value accounts only)
Download our example Personalization Rule Set Planner (linked below) and document your personalization rule sets based
on Folloze's Marketing Team's framework. Feel free to use it exactly or create your own to map out personalization rule sets,
their triggers, and content.
> Personalization Rule Set Planner Link <
Note: If you cannot access Google Documents, please reach out to academy@folloze.com for your preferred file type
Explicitly defining rules before you implement them will allow you to quickly go through approvals, remain scalable, and stay
on brand. Plus, implementation becomes a lot easier with clear guidance on what triggers a rule, who it is for, and what
changes.

Create Content that Scales
Use the Content Center to manage, store, analyze, recommend, and serve content efficiently. The Content Center helps you
keep all your materials in one place, making it easy to update and deploy content across different Boards.
Below, we show how a Board's content categories and subcategories can impact your Board's design and personalization.
Build a hierarchy for your Board's content (categories and subcategories) so you can create content sections more quickly
as well as gain more control over content in the designer for both your default Board and personalization.
Loading...
You can sort your content into categories and subcategories on your Board, like you'd organize books on shelves... For
example, you could create a category for "Customer Onboarding" with subcategories for "Videos," "Case Studies," and
"Product Guides." This setup keeps your content tidy but makes it faster to design personalized content journeys on Boards.
When you need to craft a Board for a specific stage in the buyer journey, you can quickly pull the most relevant materials
from the right shelf.
Take our Content Categories & Subcategories course for a detailed walkthrough.

Be Efficient
When you build a Folloze Board you can:
Save sections with personalization: When you apply a saved section to a Board, you can also apply all of
its personalization rules. This feature allows you to quickly replicate rule sets across multiple boards,
saving time. For example, if you have personalized banner images for seven different industries on a
Board, you can save the banner section and reuse the whole setup without having to recreate each rule.
Copy personalization rules: After creating personalization rules or rule sets, you can easily copy them to
another section or even across different boards. This streamlines the process of setting up new boards by
using pre-designed rules that fit specific campaigns, segments, or use cases.
Eılll
8
Personalize with merge fields: When possible, always opt to use merge fields over domain-level
personalization rules. You won't have to create as many individual rules, which makes personalization
more scalable.
If you haven't used these features already, practice using them in the walkthrough below.

Personalize your Board's Design
Once your default Board is complete, you can start implementing some of the personalization rules you defined in your
Personalization Rule Set Template CSV! Remember to keep the 80/20 rule and scalability in mind.
Folloze helps you facilitate targeted and efficient marketing campaigns across wide segments with tools that help you
personalize your Board's design, copy, and content. In this section, we will focus on updating your Board's design and copy.
The most common personalization rules update:
• Header Logos
• Banner Text
• Banner Images
• CTAs
As a quick refresher, please feel free to watch the how-to video below and follow along to create a few personalization rules on
a test Board.

Content Personalization Best Practices
Just like personalizing your Board's design and copy, personalizing content provides your prospects with more meaningful
experiences, fosters engagement, and guides your segments through relevant and memorable buyer journeys. Focus on
content personalization to ensure that the right information reaches buyers at the right time and caters to their specific
needs and questions at each stage of their journey.
You can personalize content in two ways on Folloze:
• In the Designer
• With the Personalization Studio
Keep these best practices in mind to provide the most relevant and engaging content to your prospects.
• Customize Content for Tier 1 Accounts: Create tailored content for your most important accounts.
• Adjust content by buyer's journey stage: Modify content based on where buyers are in their journey to ensure the
content they see is relevant to them.
• Organize your content: Put your content into clear categories and subcategories to simplify navigation and access for
both your team and your prospects.
• Create different content sections: Use a variety of content sections to break up content, draw focus to your most
popular content items, and curate a great content experience.
• Iterate based on engagement: Focus on extracting insights from content engagement and iterate quickly to improve
content (and Board) performance.

Personalize Content via the Designer
*
You can personalize content in the Designer Tab by adjusting content sections. This simple method of content
personalization is a great way to dip your toes into content personalization.
Like any personalization created in the designer, you just build a rule and adjust the design of your section. In this case,
content sections specifically. For example, you could update a single item section or a single video section. You would build
a rule and then choose a different item to show when that rule is triggered
As long as you've built your content categories and subcategories out well, you can personalize larger content sections in
interesting ways by selecting and/or deselecting specific categories for specific segments or prospects. For example, you
could have a content category with content specifically selected for prospects early in their buyer journey that includes
industry reports and white papers, etc.
Watch the video below to see how to start personalizing content by leveraging your content categories.

Personalize Content via Personalization Studio
*
Personalization Studio provides a more flexible and detailed approach to content personalization. It has a dedicated tab on
each Board, below the Designer Tab. To start using Personalization Studio, click the Personalization Tab and then click
Initialize Personalization.
Personalization Studio lets you fully control how prospects are presented with content. It is also built with rules that are
triggered by fields, like in the designer, but specifically for what content is presented. In addition, Personalization Studio lets
you easily create otherwise rules (rules that trigger for any prospects that are not in a specified group).
Note: Once Personalization Studio has been initialized, you need to make sure that all content you want everyone to see is put under the Visible to everyone rule.
This is especially important to update each time you add new content to your Board.

Create Test URLs
Create and test Board URLs before launching your Board or campaign. You can use test URLs to review the personalized
experiences you've created and see your Board the way a prospect would
You can create a personal copy of and use the Test Personalization URL Builder (linked below) to quickly create and
organize your test URLs. Or, you can create test links one by one as needed.
> Test Personalization URL Builder Link <
Note: If you cannot access Google Documents, please reach out to academy@folloze.com for your preferred file type.
To build your test URL:
1. From the Board you'd like to test, click the three vertical dots in the upper right-hand corner and then click Get Link.
2. Find your Board's Public Link and click Copy Link.
3. Paste your public link into your Test Personalization URL Builder. Use the For Links without Tokens tab if you don't see
a ? in your Board link. Use the For Links with Tokens tab if your Board has a token and/or you see a ? in your Board link.
Note: Your final test URL should be similar to:
your.subdomain.com/boardname?urlparameter=value&urlparameter2=value2
Or for tokenized links:
your.subdomain.com/boardname?token321&urlparameter=value&urlparameter2=value2
Notice that there can only ever be one question mark symbol in a URL; it documents where the URL parameters start. This is the required URL
structure for all URLS.
To test your test URL:
1. Add your Board Link to column C
2. Add your test domain value, aka UTM Domain Field, to column E
3. Click an empty cell ora cell without your test data
Copy your Full Test URL from column
. Open a brand new incognito (or private) window in your browser and navigate to your Full Test UF
Congratulations! You should now see the most recently published personalized experience for the test lead you landed as.
PROGRESs
‹ BACK
CONTINUE >
```

Create Test URLs Create and test Board URLs before launching your Board or campaign. You can use test URLs to review the ‌personalized experiences you’ve created and see your Board the way a prospect would. You can create a personal copy of and use the Test Personalization URL Builder (linked below) to quickly create and organize your test URLs. Or, you can create test links one by one as needed. > Test Personalization URL Builder Link < Note: If you cannot access Google Documents, please reach out to academy@folloze.com for your preferred file type. To build your test URL: From the Board you’d like to test, click the three vertical dots in the upper right-hand corner and then click Get Link. Find your Board’s Public Link and click Copy Link. Paste your public link into your Test Personalization URL Builder. Use the For Links without Tokens tab if you don’t see a ? in your Board link. Use the For Links with Tokens tab if your Board has a token and/or you see a ? in your Board link. Note: Your final test URL should be similar to: your.subdomain.com/boardname?urlparameter=value&urlparameter2=value2 Or for tokenized links: your.subdomain.com/boardname?token321&urlparameter=value&urlparameter2=value2 Notice that there can only ever be one question mark symbol in a URL; it documents where the URL parameters start. This is the required URL structure for all URLs. To test your test URL: Add your Board Link to column C Add your test domain value, aka UTM Domain Field, to column E Click an empty cell ora cell without your test data Copy your Full Test URL from column I Open a brand new incognito (or private) window in your browser and navigate to your Full Test URL Congratulations! You should now see the most recently published personalized experience for the test lead you landed as.
```

cookies. These data and/or cookies can directly conflict with your tests.

parameter must start with a ? and a & must precede all of the following URL parameters.

Personalization Reality Check
The B2B marketing landscape has fundamentally shifted. While many marketers have continued to optimize funnels and
perfect lead scoring, buyers have disappeared into GenAl environments like ChatGPT, Claude, and Perplexity. SEO traffic
continues to decline as the decades-old "content for contact info" model breaks down.
When buyers do reach your site, they only engage with experiences that are immediately relevant to their specific needs.
Generic content is ignored.
The stakes are clear; personalize at scale or stay invisible throughout the buyer journey. Leading B2B marketers are crafting
hyper-relevant experiences that generate deep engagement. Those who don't are getting left behind
Personalization helps you:
• Stand out from an endless barrage of generic content
• Build trust by showing that you truly understand your prospect's pain points
• Accelerate engagement by delivering relevant content and solutions
PROGRESS •
‹ BACK
CONTINUE >

Quick Wins are Real Wins
>
>
>
You don't need to overhaul everything at once. A few tactical personalization rules can significantly boost your board
engagement quickly.
That's what this course is all about. First, you'll focus on high-impact, low-effort tactics that deliver immediate results. Once
you master the fundamentals, you can scale to a more comprehensive personalization strategy as soon as you're ready.
Remember, quick wins are real wins; generally, 20% of your effort results in 80% of your results. So, get some
personalization rules in place and see the impact they make on your next campaign.
80/20 Rule

Table of Contents

Folloze Identity Model
INTRODUCTION
Before you build personalization rules, it helps to understand what you already know about your prospects and how Folloze
identifies them.
YOUR DEFAULT BOARD
PERSONALIZATION QUICK WINS
>
The Folloze Identity Model uses a progressive approach that builds prospect profiles over time through multiple
identification methods including trackable links (UTMs), identity matching, form fills, and first-party cookie tracking across
your domain (if enabled).
ACE PERSONALIZATION
>
WALK WITH PERSONALIZATION
>
Folloze's contact-level identity model can enable personalization based on many variables, from account-level (like
industry), to contact-level (like role). As prospects progress through their journey, their identity and behavior is
documented. This allows you to better understand who they are and deliver increasingly relevant experiences to them.
RIIN WITH PERSONALIZATION
CHECK YOUR UNDERSTANDING
Since lead identity is managed at the organizational level, identification on one of your company's boards automatically
becomes known across all of your boards, creating seamless, personalized experiences.
POWERED BY NORTHPASS
PROGRESS
-
11%

CONTINUE >

Data Providers
Folloze has integrations with top ABM data intelligence platforms such as 6sense [6S] and Demandbase [DB]. Your data
provider can help you segment your accounts intelligently, identify leads, give you access to unique fields to trigger
personalization, and more. If you do not have 6sense [6S] or Demandbase [DB] connected to your Folloze instance, you will
use 6signal, by 6sense, by default.
The tactics in this course mainly use Designer Personalization, one of the simplest ways to personalize boards in Folloze.
The process is straightforward; just build personalization rules.
Every personalization rule needs to answer two questions:
1. Who is this for? Select your field and field value (ie. Account Domain and google.com).
2. What changes for them? Choose which board elements to personalize (ie. banner text).
Note: Some personalization fields and field values (aka triggers) are unique to your connected data provider. These fields are marked [6S] for 6sense, or [DB] for
Demandbase. Folloze also supports Demandbase custom field mapping.
Below are the personalization rule fields used in this course. You will have access to these fields regardless of your data
provider. We've also included example field values and the associated URL parameter for domain and company name.
Field (aka Trigger)
Account Country
Account Domain
Account Name
Contact Company Name
Example Field Value
France
target.com
Target Corporation
Target
URL Parameter
dom
-
co
Note: URL parameters are a common format in which data is passed through URLs. Most marketers are familiar with UTMs such as utm_campaign, utm_source,
etc. UTMs are a specific type of URL parameter.
Later in this course, we reference buyer journey stages which can also be used to trigger personalization in Folloze.
• If you're a Demandbase user, you may know these as Journey Stages.
• If you're a 6sense user, you may know these as In-Market Stages or Buying Stages.
PROGRESS —
14%

If you do not have 6sense [6S] or Demandbase [DB] connected to your Folloze instance, you will use 6signal, by 6sense, by default. The tactics in this course mainly use Designer Personalization, one of the simplest ways to personalize boards in Folloze. The process is straightforward; just build personalization rules. Every personalization rule needs to answer two questions: Who is this for? Select your field and field value (ie. Account Domain and google.com). What changes for them? Choose which board elements to personalize (ie. banner text). Note: Some personalization fields and field values (aka triggers) are unique to your connected data provider. These fields are marked [6S] for 6sense, or [DB] for Demandbase. Folloze also supports Demandbase custom field mapping. Below are the personalization rule fields used in this course. You will have access to these fields regardless of your data provider. We’ve also included example field values and the associated URL parameter for domain and company name. Field (aka Trigger) Example Field Value URL Parameter Account Country France - Account Domain target.com dom Account Name Target Corporation - Contact Company Name Target co Note: URL parameters are a common format in which data is passed through URLs. Most marketers are familiar with UTMs such as utm_campaign, utm_source, etc. UTMs are a specific type of URL parameter. Later in this course, we reference buyer journey stages which can also be used to trigger personalization in Folloze. If you’re a Demandbase user, you may know these as Journey Stages. If you’re a 6sense user, you may know these as In-Market Stages or Buying Stages.
```

Even with personalization rules in place, your default board still matters. When you create a personalization rule, Folloze
copies the section you're personalizing so you can adjust it for your target audience. That means that anything on your
default board, including typos, outdated messaging, or placeholder content, will be copied into the personalization rule you
create. Although you can update each personalization rule at any time, updating the same issue across multiple rules can
become tedious quickly.

Journey Based Default
Your default board isn't a catch-all or a fallback. Your default board needs to help prospects educate themselves and
evaluate solutions, so don't treat it as an afterthought. The Folloze Marketing Team recommends designing your default
board for prospects in the Awareness [6S]/Qualified [DB] and Consideration [6S]/Aware [DB] stages. In other words, your top
of top-of-funnel (TOFU) audience.
This approach works because:
• Unknown visitors and early-stage accounts represent your largest segment
• Every personalization rule you build starts from this default, so getting it right saves you time and ensures consistency
Impact:
rules.
A strategic baseline experience that serves your largest segment while providing a solid foundation for personalization
How to Implement:
Step 1: Click the Al Icon at the top left of your board designer
Step 2: Click the Generate dropdown and select By Goal
Step 3: Fill out your board prompt
• In the Add some details about your board area you can add something like:
"We are a point of sale system that targets local, small, family owned cafes and coffee shops. This board is for a
promotion we are running for new customers with under 50 employees to support small businesses."
"We're running a small business promotion for our point of sale system specifically for small family owned
businesses with under 50 employees."
Step 4: Click Generate Board Text
• Your board text will be automatically generated based on your input as well as your companies Brand Voice
Management settings in Company Admin
Step 5: Review and edit or approve your board's text
PROGRESS
‹ BACK
CONTINUE
Why it Works:
By optimizing your default board for top of TOFU stage visitors, you're serving the majority of your traffic with relevant
content. From there, you can layer personalization rules on top and make targeted adjustments where they matter most.
Pro Tip: While designing your default board, note where you intuitively want to personalize for specific segments. These
insights will help guide your personalization rule strategy later on.
Note: Ask your Company Admin if they've enabled Content Al on Folloze and marked certain content as available for recommendation. More on Content Al later in
this course.
```

Dynamic Logos
*
Now that your default board has been built, let's add your first layer of personalization. We'll start with an easy, zero-
maintenance personalization feature that works for known and inferred prospects: dynamic company logos.
Time Investment: 5 minutes
Impact:
Visual personalization above the scroll with zero ongoing maintenance.
How to Implement:
Step 1: In your board's header section, click the section editor's pencil icon
Step 2: Click the Secondary logo dropdown
Step 3: Make sure to Show secondary logo
Step 4: Select Dynamic instead of Manual
That's it! Folloze automatically uses a third party tool (logo.dev) to pull in company logos for known and inferred accounts.
If you need particular versions of a logo to display for high-value accounts, we recommend creating individual
personalization rules. Read more in this help article.
Why it Works:
When prospects see their company logo, it creates an instant "this is for me" moment. This has a subtle but powerful
psychological impact.
Note: Due to the style of logos presented dynamically, we recommend keeping your header background white. Dynamic logos are often multicolor and may not be
accessible on a dark or black background.
37%

CONTINUE >

Industry-Specific Banner Images
Next, take your above-the-scroll personalization further with banner images tailored to your top target industries. Dynamic
logos say "we see you," and industry-specific imagery says "we understand your specific needs."
Time Investment: 5-10 minutes
Impact:
Imagery that immediately communicates relevance.
Build your Personalization Rule:
Step 1: Identify one of your top target industries
Step 2: Toggle on personalization at the top right of your board designer
Step 3: Click Set personalization at the top left of your banner image
Step 4: Click CREATE RULE and select Account Industry
Step 5: Check the box next to the industry you picked in Step 1 (e.g. biotech). This is your personalization rule's trigger.
• Trigger: Account Industry
• Change: Banner Image
• Example Default: Abstract banner image
• Example Change: Biotech banner image
Step 6: Click Done
• Your personalization rule should be highlighted in purple as shown below
Meet \

• If Account Industry [6S] is
agriculture
CREATE RULE

Personalized Welcome Messages
Dynamic logos and industry-specific banners create visual relevance, now let's complete our quick wins with a direct and
personalized greeting. Using merge fields your banner text transforms a generic "Welcome" into "Welcome, [Prospect
Company] team!" or "[Prospect Industry] is our priority!" without creating individual rules for each account.
Time Investment: 10 minutes
Impact:
Creates an immediate personal connection.
Build your Personalization Rule:
Step 0: Set your default! You need to set a default message that works for everyone and does not use a specific
company name or merge field (e.g., "Welcome to your Resource Hub")
Step 1: Toggle on personalization at the top right of your board designer
Step 2: Click Set personalization at the top left of your banner section
Step 3: Click CREATE RULE and select Contact Company Name
Step 4: Check the box next to any value. This is your personalization rule's trigger.
• Trigger: anv value for Contact Companv Name
• Change: Change the banner copy to include a merge field that calls out the prospect's company name if knowr
• Example Default: "Welcome to your Resource Hub"
• Example Change: "Welcome to {!contact_company)'s Resource Hub"
Step 5: Add a merge field to your banner text, for example "Welcome to {!contact_company)'s Resource Hub!"
Step 6: Click Done in the Rule builder
Why it Works:
Personalized greetings create an immediate sense of relevance and individual attention. Remember, these tactics help you
get familiar implementing personalization rules for quick impact. These tactics alone are not a replacement for unique
value driven messaging which we cover later in this course.
PROGRESS
44%
‹ BACK
CONTINUE >
Al-generated Video Agent greeting that addresses the prospect's specific industry challenges. These high-touch tactics create
executive-level experiences for your highest-value accounts.

Firmographic-Based Messaging
Firmographic-based personalization adapts your messaging to address company characteristics like employee count,
revenue, and industry. A 200-person company faces different challenges than a 10,000-person enterprise and your
messaging should reflect that you understand that.
Time Investment: 15-20 minutes
Impact:
Messaging that acknowledges prospect-specific challenges and positions your solution as the right fit for their context.
Build your Personalization Rule:
Step 1: Identify the firmographic attribute most relevant to your value proposition
Step 2: Craft a few message variations that speak to different segments
• Possible Triggers: Account Employee Range, Revenue Range, Industry
• Possible Changes: H1, H2, CTA
• Example Default H1: "Solutions for businesses at every stage."
• Example Change H1: "Compliant solutions for enterprise customers."
Why it Works:
When prospects see messaging that reflects their specific business reality, it builds immediate trust. It's important to
demonstrate that you understand their challenges and can address them. Or, even better, show how you've already
addressed the same issue(s) for companies similar to theirs.
Pro Tip: Layer multiple firmographic triggers for even more precise messaging. For example, create rules for industry specific
concerns on some sections and rules highlighting company size context on other sections on the same board. Each additional
layer of personalization increases relevance without becoming as granular as true 1-1 or requiring account specific
personalization rules.
A.C.E. Firmoaranhic-Based Personalization
PROGRESS
‹ BACK
CONTINUE >
Review your closed-won deals. Which firmographic attributes are the most common? Employee Range, Revenue Range, or
Industry? If you're unsure, start with Employee Range (SMB: <500, Mid-Market: 500-5,000, Enterprise: 5,000+). Consider
selecting your highest-revenue segment as your first target.
C - Content:
Audit your existing content, do you have relevant case studies and assets for your target segment? Identify the key
messaging difference: SMBs want speed and simplicity; enterprises need scalability and security. Ensure you have at least
one case study and one problem-focused asset for your priority segment.
E - Enact & Evaluate:
Build your first firmographic rule for your top segment. Test it, launch it, and track engagement by segment to refine your
approach.
```

Regional Content Curation
Region-specific personalization adapts your messaging based on geographic location. Good region-specific personalization
acknowledges local market conditions, compliance requirements, and regional presence. When prospects see relevant
regulatory messaging and nearby office locations, your solution feels accessible and built for their market.
>
>
Time Investment: 15-20 minutes per region
Impact:
distant.
Messaging that builds trust and relevance, demonstrates local expertise, and makes your brand feel accessible rather than
Build your Personalization Rule:
Step 1: Identify target regions that have specific needs such as the EMEA, LATAM etc.
Note: Feel free to get more specific and target the EU, or even specific countries, just be sure that the audience you're targeting truly has a unique need you can
provide value to.
Step 2: Craft a messaging that shows the value you provide to their location specific needs
• Possible Triggers: Account Country, Custom, Account Region [6S]
• Possible Changes: H1, H2, featured logos, featured case studies
• Example Default H1: "Solutions that fit your compliance needs."
• Example Change H1: "We take GDPR compliance seriously."
Why it Works:
Regional personalization overcomes "not built for my market" objections before they can form. When a EU prospect sees
other EU companies using your solution and GDPR compliance prominently displayed, you've eliminated a major trust
barrier. When an Australian buyer sees "Sydney office" instead of only US locations, your solution shifts from "foreign
vendor" to "accessible partner." This is particularly powerful in markets where prospects are skeptical of solutions built
primarily for other regions.
Pro Tip #1: For high-value markets, go beyond regional groupings and create country-specific personalization. Markets like the
United States, United Kingdom, Germany, and Australia often warrant dedicated messaging that addresses country-specific
regulations, market maturity, and competitive landscape.
Pro Tip #2: Layer region with other firmographics for precision targeting. For example, "Healthcare + EMEA" can feature GDPR-
compliant healthcare case studies. while "Financial Services + North America" hiahliahts SOC 2 compliance and US bankina
PROGRESS
633
‹ BACK
CONTINUE >
Note: Region-specific messaging differs from full localization. For true multi-language boards, many clients use Folloze's Translation Agent to quickly create 1:7
translated versions of their boards. Simply copy your board, run it through the Translation Agent, and make regional adjustments-creating a fully localized
experience in minutes. For region-based personalization within the same language, use the rules outlined here.
A.C.E. Tech Stack-Based Personalization Example
A - Audiences & Accounts:
Review where your customers and high-value prospects are located. Which regions drive the most revenue or inbound
interest? Use your data provider's regional fields to segment accounts. Prioritize regions with unique compliance
requirements or competitive dynamics.
C - Content:
Audit your content for regional relevance. Do you have customer stories from each major market? Compliance
documentation for key regulations? Prioritize creating regional case studies and regulatory content for your highest-value
markets.
E - Enact & Evaluate:
Build personalization rules for your top 2-3 regions. Test with regional URLs, launch, and compare engagement across
regions to refine your approach.

RUN with Personalization
30-45 minute multi-dimentional personalization tactics
Once you've implemented the previous personalization tactics, you're ready to combine them for even greater impact! This
is where your creativity and ability to craft compelling value propositions can make a huge impact on your account
experiences.
These more advanced tactics require clear audience segmentation. These tactics are often implemented as part of a larger
personalization strategy as discussed in the final course of the Folloze Personalization for ABX Certification:
Personalization for Growth ABM
In this section, you'll build:
• Industry-Specific Value Stories: Tailored messaging and proof points by vertical
• Journey Stage Personalization: Content and CTAs adapted to prospect buying stage
• Folloze Content Al: Al-powered personalization that does not require personalization rules
Note: Industry-specific value stories and VIP account experiences (true 1-1) are often implemented by and for enterprise companies. Individual marketers can also
use these tactics by focusing on fewer high-value accounts and leveraging Al tools to scale execution.

In addition to updating your header and banner, your team should consider investing time into crafting industry-specific
value stories. Create a cohesive narrative that demonstrates your brand's understanding of an industry's challenges,
success metrics, and terminology. This is true ABX personalization that combines multiple tactical elements into an
experience that feels purposefully built for a specific market and/or audience.

than a SaaS company. Your value story should reflect that reality across multiple board elements like messaging, proof
points, content, and calls-to-action.

Build your Value Story:
Step 1: Identify your target industry's specific challenges and unique language.
• What regulatory requirements do they need to meet?
• What metrics define success in their world?
• What terminology do they use daily?
Step 2: Map your messaging and proof points to meet their industry needs.
For example:
• Select and showcase customer logos from their industry
• Adjust above the scroll messaging to address industry-specific pain points
• Include and properly use their terminology and KPls
Step 3: Build supporting personalization rules across multiple board sections.
Why it Works:
Industry-specific value stories work because they combine the benefits of multiple personalization tactics into a unified
experience. You're highlighting relevance but more importantly, you're proving expertise.
Prospects should be able to see themselves reflected in your customers. Your messaging needs to speak their language,
and it has to address their unique challenges. Implementing industry-specific value stories helps you start working in an

A.C.E. Industry-Specific Personalization
A - Audiences & Accounts:
Identify your top 2-3 industries by revenue or strategic importance. Segment your account list by these verticals. Focus on
industries where you have strong customer success stories and clear differentiation.
C - Content:
Audit your content library by industry. Do you have enough vertical-specific assets to tell a complete story? Start with a few
relevant customer logos, industry-specific proof points, and feature an applicable brand advocate if possible. If gaps exist,
prioritize creating case studies, one-pagers or other supporting information for your focus industries.
E - Enact & Evaluate:
Build one complete industry-specific value story. Test your related personalization rules, launch your board, and monitor
engagement by industry/segment to identify which personalization has the most impact and what your investment has
provided.

Industry-Specific Value Stories In addition to updating your header and banner, your team should consider investing time into crafting industry-specific value stories. Create a cohesive narrative that demonstrates your brand's understanding of an industry's challenges, success metrics, and terminology. This is true ABX personalization that combines multiple tactical elements into an experience that feels purposefully built for a specific market and/or audience. For example, a medical device manufacturer faces different regulatory hurdles, buying committees, and ROI calculations than a SaaS company. Your value story should reflect that reality across multiple board elements like messaging, proof points, content, and calls-to-action. Time Investment: 30-40 minutes per vertical Impact: Cohesive experiences that build credibility through industry fluency and demonstrate proven success within a prospect's specific market. Build your Value Story: Step 1: Identify your target industry's specific challenges and unique language. What regulatory requirements do they need to meet? What metrics define success in their world? What terminology do they use daily? Step 2: Map your messaging and proof points to meet their industry needs. For example: Select and showcase customer logos from their industry Adjust above the scroll messaging to address industry-specific pain points Include and properly use their terminology and KPIs Step 3: Build supporting personalization rules across multiple board sections. Why it Works: Industry-specific value stories work because they combine the benefits of multiple personalization tactics into a unified experience. You're highlighting relevance but more importantly, you're proving expertise. Prospects should be able to see themselves reflected in your customers. Your messaging needs to speak their language, and it has to address their unique challenges. Implementing industry-specific value stories helps you start working in an ABX mindset. Top ABM programs operate the same way but at a more strategic and scalable program level instead of an individual board level. A.C.E. Industry-Specific Personalization A - Audiences & Accounts: Identify your top 2-3 industries by revenue or strategic importance. Segment your account list by these verticals. Focus on industries where you have strong customer success stories and clear differentiation. C - Content: Audit your content library by industry. Do you have enough vertical-specific assets to tell a complete story? Start with a few relevant customer logos, industry-specific proof points, and feature an applicable brand advocate if possible. If gaps exist, prioritize creating case studies, one-pagers or other supporting information for your focus industries. E - Enact & Evaluate: Build one complete industry-specific value story. Test your related personalization rules, launch your board, and monitor engagement by industry/segment to identify which personalization has the most impact and what your investment has provided.
```

Journey-Based Content
When building your default board earlier in this course, you targeted Awareness [6S] / Qualified [DB] and Consideration [6S]
/ Aware [DB] stage visitors aka the top of TOFU. For this section, you'll build a few personalization rules based on the
content that best engages prospects a bit lower in the TOFU.
Time Investment: 30 minutes
Impact:
Journey-based personalization increases relevance and accelerates buyer progression through the funnel by serving stage-
appropriate content that builds on their existing knowledge and interest.
Why it Works:
Journey-based personalization respects where prospects are in their research process. Rather than repeating introductory
messaging, you're serving them the most relevant content. This approach builds trust, demonstrates that you understand
their needs, and naturally guides them toward conversion-ready stages without feeling pushy. Visitors who receive journey-
appropriate content are more likely to advance to the next funnel than those who don't.
A.C.E. Journey-Based Personalization
A - Audiences & Accounts:
Review your data provider's journey stage data. Which stages have the highest account volume? Your default board already
serves Awareness/Consideration prospects. Focus your personalization efforts on Decision/Purchase stage accounts
where tailored content drives the biggest impact on conversion.
C - Content:
Map your content to buyer journey stages. Early stage needs educational content and thought leadership. Late stage needs
ROl calculators, product comparisons, and implementation guides. Audit what you have for Decision/Purchase stage
prospects. If gaps exist, prioritize creating comparison content and proof of value assets.

Why it Works: Journey-based personalization respects where prospects are in their research process. Rather than repeating introductory messaging, you're serving them the most relevant content. This approach builds trust, demonstrates that you understand their needs, and naturally guides them toward conversion-ready stages without feeling pushy. Visitors who receive journey-appropriate content are more likely to advance to the next funnel than those who don't. A.C.E. Folloze Content Al
Folloze Content Al automatically recommends the most relevant content to each visitor based on their firmographics,
engagement history, and behavior. Instead of building dozens of personalization rules, Content Al analyzes visitor signals in
real-time and surfaces the right content from your Content Center without you having to manually add each piece of content
to your board.
>
Time Investment: 10 minutes
Impact:
Scalable personalization that adapts to each visitor automatically. Your boards get smarter over time as Content Al learns
from engagement patterns holistically.
>
How to Implement:
Step 1: Navigate to your board designer
Step 2: Select or add a Content Section
Step 3: Click the Content Section's pencil icon and click the "Source" dropdown
Step 4: Select "Al" as the source (shown below)

Why it Works:
Creating personalization rules for every possible combination of industry, company size, role, and journey stage isn't
scalable. Content Al handles this complexity by analyzing multiple signals simultaneously and learning what resonates with
similar visitors. It keeps content fresh by surfacing relevant assets from your Content Center without expanding your
workload.
Curious what happens if Content Al doesn't have data on similar visitors? Don't worry, in cases where there is not enough
data to recommend relevant content, Content Al automatically adjusts to recommend content based on overall
pertormance.
A.C.E. Content Al
A - Audiences & Accounts:
Content Al works automatically for all visitors. No audience segmentation or personalization rules required. The Al analyzes
each visitor's firmographic data, engagement history, and behavioral patterns to recommend relevant content in real-time.
C - Content:
Work with your Company Admin to enable Content Al at the organization level. Mark your highest-performing assets as
"Available for recommendation" in the Content Center (summaries are auto-generated for new items). Prioritize case
studies, guides, and product sheets. The more quality content available, the smarter your recommendations become.
E - Enact & Evaluate:
Add a Content Al section to your board (Promoted Content or Single Item). Choose your Source and Journey settings
(Curated, Al, or both). Launch and monitor which content gets recommended most in your analytics.

30-Day Quick Action Plan
Start implementing personalization for your next campaign! Below is a 30-Day Quick Action Plan if you're not sure how to
get started. Start personalizing prospect experiences in ways that make an impact on your programs and campaigns.
Week 1: Build your default board. Finalize your default copy and images.
• Turn on Dynamic logos in your board's header
Week 2: Implement a few personalization rules. For example:
• Create 2 personalization rules that adjust your board's banner to use industry-specific imagery
• Adjust those 2 personalization rules to also update your board's banner copy so it addresses an industry-specific need
Week 3: Implement personalization rules that adjust content for specific people. For example, explore vertical or
geographic personalization rules to present relevant content.
Week 4: Publish a board with at least one personalization tactic from each section of this course.
1. Dynamic logos and/or logo personalization
2. Industry-specific banner images
3. Personalized welcome messages
4. Firmographic-based messaging
5. Regional content or messaging
6. Industry-specific value statement or story
7. Journey-based content or messaging

Key Takeaways
The B2B marketing landscape has fundamentally shifted. Generic experiences no longer generate engagement because
buyers are asking LLMs like ChatGPT before (or in place of) visiting vendor websites.
Build a Solid Foundation
largest segment)
• Default boards are your personalization foundation: Design for your Consideration/Awareness stage visitors (your
• Unknown visitors need love too: Your default experience serves as the baseline for all personalization rules
Start Small & Take Action
• Quick wins build confidence: Dynamic logos and industry banners deliver immediate impact
• Walk before you run: Implement individual personalization tactics before advancing to more complex strategies that
rely on multiple rulesets
• Credibility beats complexity: Simple, relevant changes often outperform elaborate rule sets
Value > Technology
• Ask the relevant question: "How does this make address my prospect's needs?"
• Relevance > sophistication: The story you tell and the value you provide is the most important ingredient in successful
personalization
• Solve real problems: Every rule should address genuine buyer needs, not just showcase capabilities
Remember the 80/20 Rule
• Impact the most people with the least effort
• Reserve account specific tactics for Tier 1 accounts
• Scale with Al assistance when appropriate
Always A.C.E. Personalization
A - Audiences & Accounts: Define your segments and understand where your prospects are in their buyer journey
C - Content: Understand what engages your audience and when in their journey it is engaging to them
E - Enact & Evaluate: Implement your personalization rules, test them, and iterate based on engagement data

Overview
Personalization is a strategy, not a feature. It's a way to increase engagement by offering the most relevant digital
experience to the buyer, considering the industry, buying stage, persona, etc.
In a 1:1 strategic account campaign, the experience is personalized to the account, as well as to the members of the buying
committee in that specific account. In 1:Few or 1:Many ABX campaigns, the experience is typically personalized to clusters
of accounts based on different attributes.
Regardless of your specific ABX program, Folloze provides you the ability to operate at scale, leveraging the ease of board
building and the straightforward way of configuring dynamic personalization rules.
In this course, you will learn how to personalize almost any element on a Folloze board without any coding, be it content,
images, or text.
We'll review the setup process and highlight the scalability and engagement benefits that personalization brings, ultimately
setting you apart from competitors.
Watch the video below for an overview of how personalization works within the Folloze platform:

Before You Get Started
• Strategic Audience Planning and Segmentation:
• Plan your audience taxonomy strategically to create effective segmentation. Identifying clusters of accounts,
focusing on common attributes like industry and buying stage. Leverage your custom account attributes by
mapping them from your MAP to Folloze.
• Optimal Personalization Fields:
• Choose 2-3 fields for personalization using the 80/20 rule to maintain a balance. Creativity and value-driven
messaging, considering the customer's perspective, enhance the impact of personalized content.
• Plan for the Default:
• Build personalization rules from a strong default Board to ensure that every visitor to your Board will have an
impactful experience, whether they see the default or personalized version of the Board

Known v. Unknown Visitors
• Known (generally outbound, like email campaigns or Sales outreach):

• Unknown (generally inbound, like Targeted Ads or Social posts):
• Visitors landing on a Board with no individual identifying information

Activity: Personalize your Board Design
Your turn! Personalize your Academy Test Board design by following the steps below:
*
>
1. Using your Academy Test Board, click the gear icon and click Enable Personalization in General Settings.
2. Go to the Designer tab and toggle on Personalization.
3. Click Set Personalization for the Banner section on your Board.
4. Click Create Rule and select Account Domain as the attribute to personalize by.
5. Click Add Account Domain and type the domain you'd like to personalize for - for this example, type folloze.com.
6. Click the Background Editor and add a unique background image that differs from your main page.
7. Use the Text Editor to customize the title to "This is My Personalized Board".
8. Publish these changes.
Note: Personalization rules can also be set for a Contact Card on a Board. For more information on Contact Cards and personalization
rules, see this Help Center article.

Activity: Personalize your Content
Your turn! Personalize your Academy Test Board content by following the steps below:
INTRODUCTION
DESIGN PERSONALIZATION
CONTENT PERSONALIZATION
TEST YOUR PERSONALIZATION RULES
1. Navigate to the Personalization Studio tab.
2. Click Create rules block and select Account Domain.
3. Click Add Account Domain and type the domain you'd like to personalize for - for this example, type folloze.com.
4. Drag and drop content items that will only be visible to visitors from folloze.com into the new rules block.
5. Drag and drop all other content from your Board that should be visible to everyone into the Visible to everyone box.
6. Click Save Rules.
Note: If the Visible to everyone box is empty, no content will be presented to the default group

Activity: Test your Personalization Rules
Your turn! Test the personalization rules that you've created on your Academy Test Board by following the steps below:
1. Click the 3 dots in the top right corner.
2. Select Get Link.
3. From the Public Link section, click Copy Link.
4. Open an incongito window and paste the link into the browser (do not hit enter yet).
5. Reference the parameters below and append the end of this link to include: ?[attribute]=[value], for this example, your
link should be [public link]?dom=folloze.com
6. Hit enter on your browser to view the personalized version of the Board.
Note: if the public link to your Board is a tokenized link, append this format instead: [attribute]=[value]
The attributes should be appended for the link to test as follows (replace brackets with the applicable value):
Email: ?em=[value]
First Name: ?fn=[value]
Last Name: ?In=[value]
Role/Title: ?ro=[valuel
Phone Number: ?ph=[value]
Company: ?co=[value]
Industry: ?ind=[value]
Location: ?loc=[value]
Account Domain: ?dom=[value]
Inviter's Email: ?inby=[value]
-

Activity: Test your Personalization Rules Your turn! Test the personalization rules that you've created on your Academy Test Board by following the steps below: Click the 3 dots in the top right corner. Select Get Link. From the Public Link section, click Copy Link. Open an incongito window and paste the link into the browser (do not hit enter yet). Reference the parameters below and append the end of this link to include: ?[attribute]=[value], for this example, your link should be [public link]?dom=folloze.com Hit enter on your browser to view the personalized version of the Board. Note: if the public link to your Board is a tokenized link, append this format instead: &[attribute]=[value] The attributes should be appended for the link to test as follows (replace brackets with the applicable value): Email: ?em=[value] First Name: ?fn=[value] Last Name: ?ln=[value] Role/Title: ?ro=[value] Phone Number: ?ph=[value] Company: ?co=[value] Industry: ?ind=[value] Location: ?loc=[value] Account Domain: ?dom=[value] Inviter's Email: ?inby=[value]

Foundations of Modern ABX Measurement
First, let's review how buyer-to-buyer (B2B) buying has evolved.
Traditional measurement approaches no longer match today's complex B2B buying environment, where it is common for
over 20 stakeholders to collaborate in buying committees and where 70-80% of the journey happens digitally (before sales
engagement).
Most organizations still rely on "rearview mirror metrics," aka lagging indicators, that only reveal problems when it's too late
to course-correct. A shift from Account-Based Marketing (ABM) to Account-Based Experiences (ABX) can help provide
earlier signals that allow for timely adjustments.
Hear why it's important to adopt a forward-looking measurement framework in the video below featuring Melinda Monaco,
Senior Director of Revenue Marketing & Operations at Folloze.

Before you Get Started
When implementing ABX programs, it's critical to balance two main types of metrics: predictive indicators and validation
metrics.
Predictive indicators forecast future outcomes by tracking which accounts engage across channels, how many
stakeholders show interest, what content resonates, and how engagement patterns evolve. These leading metrics signal
pipeline development months ahead, enabling proactive resource allocation before gaps form.
Validation metrics confirm whether these predictive signals translate to business outcomes (e.g., pipeline creation,
opportunity advancement, closed revenue). The Folloze Impact Dashboard captures essential first-party engagement
signals based on actual behavioral data that shows real buying intent.
Combine predictive indicators with validation metrics to create a powerful measurement framework that transforms
reactive reporting into a proactive strategy.
When teams implement this balanced measurement approach, they can predict pipeline development months in advance,
allowing real-time strategy adjustments before problems materialize.
To get started, you'll need access to these key systems:
• ABM platform (e.g., 6sense, Demandbase) for intent signals and reach
• Folloze Impact Dashboard for first-party engagement data
• Customer Relationship Management (CRM) software for pipeline and revenue validation
Before diving into these more advanced measurement principles, ensure you have the fundamentals in place. If you're not
familiar with account-based deployment models, start with our Outperform Demand Generation Expectations with ABX
course.
Note: No ABM platform? No problem. We recommend alternative measurement options throughout this course.
Watch a quick overview of predictive indicators and validation metrics and how they're better together below.

Scale: Reach Market Potential (primarily leading indicators)
• Account reach and coverage serve as early predictive signals
• These metrics forecast future pipeline potential by measuring your market penetration
• Essential metrics include total accounts reached, coverage rate, and engagement depth
Engage: Measure Touchpoint Effectiveness (primarily leading indicators)
• Engagement depth and quality act as predictors of future conversion
• Engagement patterns help anticipate pipeline development months in advance
• Essential metrics include engagement rate, content consumption, and time spent
Impact: Connect Engagement to Business Impact (combining leading and lagging indicators)
• Ties predictive engagement signals to pipeline and revenue outcomes
• Validates the predictive power of your leading indicators
• Creates a complete picture of program effectiveness
• Essential metrics include sales-ready accounts, pipeline influenced, and revenue impact
You'll download your own ABX Performance Tracker template with straightforward ways to apply these principles,
regardless of your company's tech stack.
Note: Although various metrics can be included (determined by program goals), we provide essential metrics for each principle so you and your team can get
started quickly.

The Power of Baseline Understanding
Starting with accurate baseline measurements isn't just good practice, it's essential for ABX success.
When implementing measurement frameworks, start small and build momentum to consistently deliver the best results.
Baseline data serves as your program's compass, providing crucial visibility into your current market position and
engagement effectiveness. Without these foundational metrics, you're essentially flying blind in your ABX journey.
Even imperfect baseline data is infinitely better than none. It transforms abstract goals like "improved engagement" into
concrete, measurable targets like "increase target account engagement from 17% to 30%."
Hear more from Melinda Monaco about how establishing baseline measurements can transform program performance
discussions into data-driven decisions in the video below.
Loading...
This data-first approach helps you set realistic objectives while identifying immediate opportunities for impact, such as
discovering unexpectedly high engagement rates in certain industry segments.
PROGRESS -
10%
‹ BACK
CONTINUE >
performance, you can demonstrate progress through concrete metrics and trends. This evidence-based approach
transforms conversations from subjective debates about strategy into objective analyses of results, enabling smarter
decisions about resource allocation. When executives see that target account engagement has increased by 22% quarter-
over-quarter, budget conversations become much easier.

Remember Early-Stage Accounts
The pursuit of market coverage often leads companies down conventional paths that can inadvertently limit their growth
potential. Test and challenge your conventional marketing assumptions to unlock growth opportunities.
When Folloze's Marketing Team adjusted their focus beyond the usual enterprise targets and started including early-stage
accounts (previously dismissed as "under a rock") they greatly increased market coverage, jumping from 9% to 56%!
Hear from Folloze marketing leadership as they share a compelling case study that reveals the true potential of their
addressable market in the video below.

Scale & Engagement Sweet Spot
In today's competitive B2B landscape, marketers face the perpetual challenge of balancing quality engagement with
scale.
Folloze's Marketing Team confronted this exact dilemma head-on during their year-long ABX implementation journey. This
comprehensive case study reveals how they successfully expanded reach from 250 accounts to over 13,700 while
discovering the critical "sweet spot" that maximizes both reach and meaningful engagement.
Through strategic segmentation, multi-channel coordination, and content optimization, Folloze uncovered actionable
insights that transformed their account-based approach and delivered exceptional results.
Hear Folloze marketing leadership as they share how strategic engagement tactics transformed their ABX program in the video
below.

Measure what Matters
Unlock your engagement data with these four metrics:
• Engagement Rate
• Content Consumption
• Time Spent
• Account Engagement Scores
These metrics are your ABX program's vital signs. Monitor them to see if your campaigns are hitting the mark, which
accounts are ready for sales, and when to serve up the right content at the right time.
Combine insights from your ABM platform and the Folloze Impact Dashboard to:
• Spot buying signals early
• Know when accounts are sales-ready
• Create content that resonates
• Time your outreach perfectly
• Show clear pipeline impact
Dive into each metric and learn how they work together to supercharge your ABX performance.
Walk through the four key metrics that matter for tracking engagement effectively with Melinda Monaco in the video below.

Tell Compelling Data Stories
Data visualization is just the beginning, your metrics need to tell a compelling narrative. When crafted thoughtfully, data
storytelling transforms raw numbers into clear evidence of strategic impact. Rather than simply reporting engagement rates
or pipeline metrics, you should demonstrate how your team deepens buyer relationships and adapts to complex purchasing
behaviors.
This narrative approach doesn't just inform, it builds confidence in marketing's ability to drive measurable business
outcomes and unites teams around shared goals.
Hear from Melinda Monaco as she shows you how to craft stakeholder-specific narratives about your engagement metrics that
drive action.

From Engagement to Revenue
Every B2B marketer faces the ultimate challenge: proving that engagement translates to revenue. Folloze's Marketing Team
shares their journey from fragmented data and unclear return on investment (ROI) to a comprehensive measurement
framework that definitively connects ABX efforts to business impact.
By unifying their tech stack and implementing structured progression tracking, they achieved remarkable results, including a
25% growth in surging accounts, an 80% increase in monthly meetings, 4X growth in influenced opportunities, and a 6X
boost in monthly revenue impact.
Hear from Folloze marketing leadership about how a structured measurement framework that connects ABX engagement to
revenue impact can help prove ROI across marketing initiatives.
